 % Abstract — Depth Fingerprinting of Recursive LLM Generation Chains
 \begin{abstract}
-Recursive application of language models---where each generation step conditions on prior LLM output---produces text with progressively altered distributional properties.
-We study this phenomenon under controlled conditions, restricting to base (pretrained) models to isolate the generation mechanism from instruction-tuning effects.
-We formulate \emph{ordinal depth estimation}---classifying text as human-written (gen-0), first-generation (gen-1), or deeper recursive output (gen-2+)---and characterize a \emph{differential depth response}: central-tendency features drift monotonically with generation depth, while distributional shape features remain near-stationary, consistent with recursive generation inducing a location shift in the token-level NLL distribution without altering its functional form.
-A 7-feature ensemble exploiting this complementarity achieves binary F1\,=\,0.723 for gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+ depth discrimination across six generators from three model families ($p < 0.001$ vs.\ all baselines).
-We additionally report that depth-aware data curation does not improve downstream language model quality over random subsampling at equal sample count.
+When LLM outputs re-enter generation pipelines, each recursive step compresses \emph{where} text sits in distributional space while leaving \emph{how} it is shaped nearly unchanged.
+We formalize this asymmetry as \emph{differential depth response} (DDR) and characterize it across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B parameters): under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.9$, $\tau \leq 0.7$), NLL location statistics and lexical diversity drift monotonically with generation depth (Cohen's $d{=}0.76$--$1.17$), while distributional shape features remain near-stationary ($d < 0.10$)---a separation validated by five independent effect-size metrics and data-driven clustering (silhouette\,=\,0.632).
+Where prior work on iterative generation documents \emph{what converges}, the novel observation here is \emph{what is preserved}: shape invariance under the same process that compresses location provides the stable reference needed for depth estimation without per-generator calibration.
+Exploiting DDR, a 7-feature ensemble achieves leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694 (95\% CI [.688, .700]) under leave-one-generator-out evaluation and 0.830 on heterogeneous cross-model chains, matching multi-detector ensembles at 2.4$\times$ lower inference cost.
+The signal attenuates under instruction tuning; current applicability is restricted to base models.
 \end{abstract}

+\vspace{2pt}
+\noindent\begin{minipage}{\columnwidth}
+\scriptsize $^{\ast}$OLMo temperature experiments use OLMo-2-0425-1B (April 2025 release); the original OLMo-2-1B-hf weights are no longer publicly available.\par\vspace{1pt}
+$^{\dagger}$Pythia-2.8B and Qwen3-1.7B use 6-generator LOGO (pre-expansion); OLMo-2-1B and Qwen3-0.6B use 8-generator LOGO. Within-model comparisons are unaffected.
+\end{minipage}
+
+\begin{sloppypar}
+Temperature sensitivity is asymmetric and model-dependent. High-temperature degradation ($t{=}0.9$) is universal: all four generators lose $9.3$--$26.0$\,pp in binary F1, with entropy mean's $|\rho|$ weakening to $0.26$--$0.37$ (vs.\ $0.58$--$0.72$ at $t{=}0.5$). Low-temperature improvement ($t{=}0.5$) is model-dependent: Pythia-2.8B and OLMo-2-1B gain $+7.4$ and $+8.8$\,pp respectively, while both Qwen models show negligible change ($0$--$2.0$\,pp). This split tracks model family rather than scale: Qwen3-0.6B (smallest) and Qwen3-1.7B both plateau, while OLMo-2-1B (similar scale to Qwen3-1.7B) benefits substantially. One possible explanation is that Qwen models produce more peaked output distributions at $t{=}0.7$, leaving less room for low-temperature compression to sharpen the depth signal.
+\end{sloppypar}
 
-The depth signal is robust to moderate sampling variation: low and default configurations differ by at most 0.3\,pp in both 3-class and binary F1, and entropy--depth Spearman correlations remain strongly negative ($\rho \leq -0.60$).
-At high temperature ($t{=}1.0$, top-$p{=}0.95$), classification degrades by approximately 6\,pp and the entropy--depth correlation collapses to near zero ($\rho = -0.002$).
-This is consistent with the hypothesis that depth features are shaped by the sampling mechanism (\S\ref{sec:discussion}): elevated temperature injects sufficient randomness to flatten the distributional drift that accumulates across generation steps, effectively masking the depth signal.
-The practical implication is that depth fingerprinting applies reliably under standard decoding regimes but should not be expected to generalize to high-entropy sampling settings.
+The method operates reliably within $t \in [0.5, 0.7]$ across all tested models. Degradation onset at $t{=}0.9$ defines the practical upper bound, consistent with the hypothesis that elevated temperature injects sufficient randomness to flatten the distributional drift that DDR exploits (\S\ref{sec:discussion}).
+
+\subsection*{Per-Generator Performance}
+
+Figure~\ref{fig:per-gen} visualizes per-generator LOGO performance.
+Pythia models (1B, 2.8B) achieve highest F1, while larger models (Mistral-7B, Qwen3-8B) show degradation.
+The scale effect (larger models $\rightarrow$ lower F1) is consistent with higher-capacity models generating text statistically closer to human writing.
+
+\begin{figure}[H]
+\centering
+\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig_appendix_per_gen.pdf}
+\caption{Per-generator LOGO performance. (a) 8-generator binary and 3-class F1, sorted by binary F1. Dashed lines: pool average. (b) 6-generator LOGO detail heatmap showing the performance spread across model families.}
+\label{fig:per-gen}
+\end{figure}

LOGO evaluation; 95\\% bootstrap CIs.$^{\\ast}$$^{\\dagger}$}\n \\label{tab:sampling-ablation}\n \\small\n-\\begin{tabular}{lcc ccc}\n+\\resizebox{\\linewidth}{!}{%\n+\\begin{tabular}{llcc c}\n \\toprule\n-Config & Temp.\\ & Top-$p$ & 3-class F1 & Binary F1 & $\\rho$(entropy\\_mean) \\\\\n+Model & Temp & LOGO Binary F1 & 95\\% CI & $\\rho$(ent.\\ mean) \\\\\n \\midrule\n-Low        & 0.5 & 0.80 & 0.785 & 0.797 & $-0.694$ \\\\\n-Default    & 0.7 & 0.90 & 0.788 & 0.799 & $-0.602$ \\\\\n-High       & 1.0 & 0.95 & 0.726 & 0.733 & $-0.002$ \\\\\n+Pythia-2.8B$^{\\dagger}$ & 0.5  & \\textbf{0.814} & [.798, .828] & $-0.58$ \\\\\n+Pythia-2.8B$^{\\dagger}$ & 0.7  & 0.740 & [.725, .755] & $-0.60$ \\\\\n+Pythia-2.8B$^{\\dagger}$ & 0.9  & 0.480 & [.465, .498] & $-0.30$ \\\\\n+\\midrule\n+OLMo-2-1B & 0.5  & \\textbf{0.783} & [.768, .798] & $-0.72$ \\\\\n+OLMo-2-1B & 0.7  & 0.695 & [.679, .711] & $-0.60$ \\\\\n+OLMo-2-1B & 0.9  & 0.453 & [.437, .469] & $-0.26$ \\\\\n+\\midrule\n+Qwen3-1.7B$^{\\dagger}$ & 0.5  & 0.751 & --- & $-0.60$ \\\\\n+Qwen3-1.7B$^{\\dagger}$ & 0.7  & 0.751 & --- & $-0.60$ \\\\\n+Qwen3-1.7B$^{\\dagger}$ & 0.9  & 0.566 & --- & $-0.37$ \\\\\n+\\midrule\n+Qwen3-0.6B & 0.5  & 0.711 & [.694, .729] & $-0.67$ \\\\\n+Qwen3-0.6B & 0.7  & 0.691 & [.673, .709] & $-0.60$ \\\\\n+Qwen3-0.6B & 0.9  & 0.598 & [.582, .615] & $-0.29$ \\\\\n \\bottomrule\n-\\end{tabular}\n+\\end{tabular}}\n \\end{table}\n+\\vspace{2pt}\n+\\noindent\\begin{minipage}{\\columnwidth}\n+\\scriptsize $^{\\ast}$OLMo temperature experiments use OLMo-2-0425-1B (April 2025 release); the original OLMo-2-1B-hf weights are no longer publicly available.\\par\\vspace{1pt}\n+$^{\\dagger}$Pythia-2.8B and Qwen3-1.7B use 6-generator LOGO (pre-expansion); OLMo-2-1B and Qwen3-0.6B use 8-generator LOGO. Within-model comparisons are unaffected.\n+\\end{minipage}\n+\n+\\begin{sloppypar}\n+Temperature sensitivity is asymmetric and model-dependent. High-temperature degradation ($t{=}0.9$) is universal: all four generators lose $9.3$--$26.0$\\,pp in binary F1, with entropy mean's $|\\rho|$ weakening to $0.26$--$0.37$ (vs.\\ $0.58$--$0.72$ at $t{=}0.5$). Low-temperature improvement ($t{=}0.5$) is model-dependent: Pythia-2.8B and OLMo-2-1B gain $+7.4$ and $+8.8$\\,pp respectively, while both Qwen models show negligible change ($0$--$2.0$\\,pp). This split tracks model family rather than scale: Qwen3-0.6B (smallest) and Qwen3-1.7B both plateau, while OLMo-2-1B (similar scale to Qwen3-1.7B) benefits substantially. One possible explanation is that Qwen models produce more peaked output distributions at $t{=}0.7$, leaving less room for low-temperature compression to sharpen the depth signal.\n+\\end{sloppypar}\n \n-The depth signal is robust to moderate sampling variation: low and default configurations differ by at most 0.3\\,pp in both 3-class and binary F1, and entropy--depth Spearman correlations remain strongly negative ($\\rho \\leq -0.60$).\n-At high temperature ($t{=}1.0$, top-$p{=}0.95$), classification degrades by approximately 6\\,pp and the entropy--depth correlation collapses to near zero ($\\rho = -0.002$).\n-This is consistent with the hypothesis that depth features are shaped by the sampling mechanism (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}): elevated temperature injects sufficient randomness to flatten the distributional drift that accumulates across generation steps, effectively masking the depth signal.\n-The practical implication is that depth fingerprinting applies reliably under standard decoding regimes but should not be expected to generalize to high-entropy sampling settings.\n+The method operates reliably within $t \\in [0.5, 0.7]$ across all tested models. Degradation onset at $t{=}0.9$ defines the practical upper bound, consistent with the hypothesis that elevated temperature injects sufficient randomness to flatten the distributional drift that DDR exploits (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).\n+\n+\\subsection*{Per-Generator Performance}\n+\n+Figure~\\ref{fig:per-gen} visualizes per-generator LOGO performance.\n+Pythia models (1B, 2.8B) achieve highest F1, while larger models (Mistral-7B, Qwen3-8B) show degradation.\n+The scale effect (larger models $\\rightarrow$ lower F1) is consistent with higher-capacity models generating text statistically closer to human writing.\n+\n+\\begin{figure}[H]\n+\\centering\n+\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig_appendix_per_gen.pdf}\n+\\caption{Per-generator LOGO performance. (a) 8-generator binary and 3-class F1, sorted by binary F1. Dashed lines: pool average. _source line 2327 · 2026-05-20T19:43:42.207Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_015aT8AFA2kDCweZtTZXCCuN`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related}
3	
4	\subsection{AI-Generated Text Detection}
5	\label{sec:related-detection}
6	
7	The detection literature spans statistical tests \cite{gltr,grover,release_strategies}, zero-shot scoring \cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,dna_gpt}, and learned classifiers \cite{ghostbuster,feat_aug_transformer,reliable_detection,ld_score}; see \citet{wu_survey_2025} and \citet{jawahar_survey} for surveys.
8	RAID \cite{raid} benchmarks binary detection across generators and attacks; we extend the task from binary to ordinal depth estimation.
9	Several recent efforts move beyond the binary boundary---collaboration roles \cite{beyond_binary}, joint detection-attribution \cite{two_birds}, creator-editor modes \cite{beyond_final_actor}, multi-generator attribution \cite{semeval2024_task8}---but all taxonomize by \emph{source} or \emph{production mode}, not recursive generation \emph{depth}; repurposing their scores as depth predictors reveals high cross-generator variance (binary F1\,=\,0.52--0.71; Table~\ref{tab:baselines}) \cite{paraphrase_evades,detection_possibilities}.
10	Our contribution is orthogonal: the task formulation itself (ordinal depth classification \cite{gutierrez_ordinal_survey,coral_ordinal,kasa_ordinality_nlp}) and the finding that drifting and stationary features together yield consistent cross-generator performance where individual detection scores do not \cite{uchendu_attribution,munir_looking_glass}.
11	
12	\subsection{Model Collapse and Iterative Generation}
13	\label{sec:related-collapse}
14	
15	Self-consuming generative models progressively lose distributional coverage \cite{self_consuming_mad,curse_of_recursion,seddik_synthetic_data}, though data mixing mitigates collapse \cite{model_collapse_avoidable,strong_model_collapse}.
16	These studies address \emph{training-loop} degradation where parameter updates erode tail coverage; we study \emph{inference chains} where parameters are fixed and only the input distribution evolves.
17	Two concurrent works are most directly relevant: \citet{broken_telephone} analyze paraphrasing and translation chains, documenting progressive information loss, while \citet{markovian_chains} demonstrate attractor dynamics that bound distinguishable depth to approximately three classes; \citet{attractor_cycles} provide complementary evidence that LLM outputs converge to cyclic attractor states under repeated generation.
18	We study \emph{non-paraphrastic continuation} chains, where the absence of semantic anchoring permits richer depth signal, and offer a complementary decomposition: where these works characterize \emph{that} and \emph{why} convergence occurs, DDR isolates \emph{what} converges (NLL location) from what is preserved (NLL shape), enabling ordinal classification without per-generator calibration.
19	The independent convergence of all three approaches on ${\sim}$3 distinguishable depth classes---via Markovian dynamics \cite{markovian_chains}, information-theoretic loss \cite{broken_telephone}, and distributional feature compression (this work)---points to a shared resolution ceiling in recursive text generation.
20	
21	\subsection{Vocabulary Diversity and Stylometry}
22	\label{sec:related-diversity}
23	
24	Declining lexical diversity in LLM text is documented in both training \cite{curious_decline} and deployment settings \cite{writing_diversity}.
25	Our surface features (TTR, hapax ratio) are classical stylometric measures \cite{stamatatos_survey,koppel_authorship}; we repurpose them from generator identity \cite{uchendu_attribution} to the depth dimension, where their monotonic decline under top-$p$ sampling \cite{nucleus_sampling} constitutes a drifting component of DDR.
26	
27	\subsection{Data Quality and Provenance}
28	\label{sec:related-provenance}
29	
30	Scalable data curation relies on quality signals \cite{dsir,qurating,ccnet}; generation depth offers a complementary provenance axis.
31	Proactive watermarking \cite{watermark_llm,synthid_text,aaronson_watermark} embeds identifiable patterns but degrades across multi-step chains \cite{chainwash}, motivating passive approaches.
32	Our NLL-based analysis connects to \citet{min_k_percent} (probability-tail membership inference) but extends from binary membership to full distributional decomposition for ordinal depth.
33	\citet{koike_mia_detection} formalize this connection from the opposite direction, showing that detection scores implicitly perform membership inference, underscoring the shared statistical basis of detection and provenance.
34	
```

</details>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	AI text detection asks a single binary question---\emph{human or machine?}---and recent methods answer it with increasing reliability \cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,ld_score}.
5	Yet this framing elides a richer structure emerging on the open web: as LLM outputs re-enter training corpora \cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \emph{recursive depths}---an estimated 7--17\% of recent scientific publications already contain substantial LLM-modified content \cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}---and each generation cycle pushes distributional properties further from both the human source and the first-generation predecessor.
6	Knowing \emph{how many times} text has been recursively generated matters for data provenance \cite{ccnet,dsir} and model collapse analysis \cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}, yet no current method moves beyond the binary boundary.
7	
8	We introduce \emph{depth fingerprinting}: classifying text by ordinal generation depth (gen-0\,/\,gen-1\,/\,gen-2+) and decomposing the distributional drift that accumulates across depths into structurally distinct components.
9	The core empirical finding is a \emph{differential depth response} (DDR): across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B), the NLL distribution's central tendency (mean entropy, lexical diversity) drifts monotonically with depth while its shape (entropy extremes, tail structure) remains an order of magnitude more stable ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$ vs.\ $d < 0.10$; five independent effect-size metrics converge on this partition).
10	This location/shape asymmetry exposes a structural property of fixed-parameter autoregressive sampling: the decoding mechanism systematically compresses where the output distribution sits while preserving its higher-order form---a pattern mechanistically distinct from the tail-coverage erosion in model collapse \cite{curse_of_recursion}, and analytically informative independent of classification accuracy.
11	Concurrent work characterizes convergence in paraphrastic and translation chains \cite{broken_telephone,markovian_chains}; ours is the first to decompose the drift into complementary location and shape components and to leverage this asymmetry for cross-generator depth prediction.
12	We restrict to base (pretrained) models to isolate the generation dynamics from alignment artifacts (\S\ref{sec:discussion}).
13	
14	As a proof of concept, a lightweight 7-feature ensemble informed by DDR achieves binary F1\,=\,0.694 under leave-one-generator-out evaluation and 0.830 on heterogeneous cross-model chains, surpassing individual detection baselines by 6.7--36.3\,pp while requiring 2.4$\times$ less inference cost than multi-detector ensembles of comparable accuracy.
15	
16	Our contributions:
17	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
18	    \item \textbf{Empirical decomposition (DDR).} Data-driven clustering ($k{=}2$, silhouette\,=\,0.632) reveals that inference-chain drift separates into a drifting location component ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) and a stable shape component ($d < 0.10$). Where prior work documents \emph{what converges}, DDR identifies \emph{what is preserved}---enabling depth detection without per-generator calibration.
19	    \item \textbf{Task formulation and evaluation suite.} We define generation depth classification as an ordinal prediction task and construct a controlled dataset of 37K texts from eight generators across five families (0.6B--8B), with leave-one-generator-out cross-validation as the primary evaluation protocol.
20	    \item \textbf{Proof-of-concept classifier with boundary analysis.} A 7-feature ensemble achieves binary F1\,=\,0.830 on cross-model chains; we systematically map its operating boundaries across temperature, prompt type, instruction tuning, and model scale.
21	\end{enumerate}
22	
```

```text
1	% Abstract — Depth Fingerprinting of Recursive LLM Generation Chains
2	\begin{abstract}
3	When LLM outputs re-enter generation pipelines, each recursive step compresses \emph{where} text sits in distributional space while leaving \emph{how} it is shaped nearly unchanged.
4	We formalize this asymmetry as \emph{differential depth response} (DDR) and characterize it across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B parameters): under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.9$, $\tau \leq 0.7$), NLL location statistics and lexical diversity drift monotonically with generation depth (Cohen's $d{=}0.76$--$1.17$), while distributional shape features remain near-stationary ($d < 0.10$)---a separation validated by five independent effect-size metrics and data-driven clustering (silhouette\,=\,0.632).
5	Where prior work on iterative generation documents \emph{what converges}, the novel observation here is \emph{what is preserved}: shape invariance under the same process that compresses location provides the stable reference needed for depth estimation without per-generator calibration.
6	Exploiting DDR, a 7-feature ensemble achieves leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694 (95\% CI [.688, .700]) under leave-one-generator-out evaluation and 0.830 on heterogeneous cross-model chains, matching multi-detector ensembles at 2.4$\times$ lower inference cost.
7	The signal attenuates under instruction tuning; current applicability is restricted to base models.
8	\end{abstract}
9	
```

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	Recursive generation leaves a systematic distributional signature: central-tendency features drift monotonically with depth while shape features remain an order of magnitude more stable---a dichotomy we formalize as differential depth response (DDR), validated by five independent effect-size metrics across eight generators from five model families.
5	This decomposition enables cross-generator depth classification (leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694 under LOGO, 0.830 on cross-model chains) and provides an analytical lens complementary to model collapse: fixed-parameter sampling compresses distributional location while preserving shape, whereas retraining erodes both.
6	Current boundaries are instruction tuning---where alignment reshapes the generation attractor---and frontier scale, where preliminary 14B evidence confirms the DDR partition with attenuated effect sizes; 70B+ models remain untested.
7	
```

```text
1	\section{Results}
2	\label{sec:results}
3	
4	\subsection{Depth Classification Performance}
5	\label{sec:results-classification}
6	
7	Before analyzing the DDR decomposition (\S\ref{sec:results-differential}), we establish that generation depth is classifiable across diverse architectures.
8	The 7-feature ensemble achieves 3-class LOGO F1\,=\,0.762 (95\% CI [0.758, 0.766]; $N$\,=\,37{,}058) across eight generators from five families (Table~\ref{tab:logo-results}).
9	Because gen-0 seeds are shared across generators (\S\ref{sec:generation}), gen-0 classification is near-perfect (per-class F1\,$\approx$\,0.94), inflating the 3-class metric.
10	We therefore adopt \textbf{leak-free binary F1} (gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+)\,=\,0.694 (CI [0.688, 0.700]) as the primary metric throughout: it excludes gen-0 entirely and is unaffected by seed sharing.
11	Per-generator binary F1 ranges from 0.633 (Mistral-7B) to 0.724 (Pythia-2.8B), with the primary difficulty concentrated at the gen-1\,/\,gen-2+ boundary (Appendix~\ref{app:confusion}).
12	
13	\paragraph{Cross-model generalization.}
14	Cross-model chains (randomly selected generator per step) yield binary F1\,=\,0.830 (95\% CI [.818, .841]) under 5-generator LOGO.\footnote{5-generator subset (Pythia-2.8B/1B, OLMo-2-1B, Qwen3-0.6B/1.7B); generator-level LOGO with bootstrap CIs ($n$\,=\,2{,}000). Not directly comparable to 8-generator LOGO due to differing subsets.}
15	Single-generator transfer (Appendix~\ref{app:transfer}) confirms the signal is not a mixed-chain artifact: classifiers trained on one generator transfer to all others with mean binary F1\,=\,0.810; LOFO yields 0.686.
16	A scale effect emerges: Pythia achieves the highest F1 (0.718--0.724) while larger models degrade (Mistral-7B: 0.633), partly reflecting same-family affinity with the Pythia-410M reference (cross-reference spread $\leq$0.013; \S\ref{sec:results-differential}); cross-domain signal persists with variation (Appendix~\ref{app:domain}).
17	An end-to-end QLoRA classifier ($+$11.6\,pp, 3-class F1\,=\,0.905; Appendix~\ref{app:qlora}) confirms that richer representations capture additional depth signal; on instruct data, QLoRA recovers to 0.89--0.91 (Appendix~\ref{app:instruct}), establishing the empirical decomposition---not the specific classifier---as DDR's primary contribution.
18	
19	\paragraph{Length confound control.}
20	Length-matched subsampling yields binary F1\,=\,0.724 (vs.\ 0.770 unmatched); a random-subsample control confirms the drop reflects deconfounding, not sample-size reduction (Appendix~\ref{app:length}).
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_012jw19fxEh3en8h7AJpZ5z1`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	Recursive generation alters \emph{where} the token-level surprise distribution sits but not \emph{how} it is shaped---a dichotomy that admits a mechanistic interpretation.
5	Under nucleus sampling, top-$p$ truncation selects from a fixed probability-mass fraction at each step, preserving the functional form of the conditional distribution regardless of input content.
6	What changes across recursive steps is the \emph{input} to this truncation: as the model conditions on its own increasingly self-typical outputs, the expected token-level NLL compresses monotonically---each generation feeds slightly more predictable input into the same shape-preserving mechanism.
7	This account yields two testable predictions: (1)~increasing sampling randomness should weaken the location drift by disrupting mode-seeking, and (2)~semantic-preservation constraints should attenuate it by anchoring the input distribution.
8	Both are empirically confirmed: at $t{=}1.0$, the location signal collapses ($\rho$: $-0.60 \to -0.002$; Appendix~\ref{app:sampling-sensitivity}); under elaboration prompts, drift weakens relative to continuation (F1\,=\,0.730 vs.\ 0.781; Appendix~\ref{app:prompt-type}), consistent with \citet{broken_telephone}, while cross-type transfer remains viable (F1\,=\,0.753).
9	Permutation importance, Granger causality, and feature intervention analyses provide further causal evidence: drifting features contribute $5.5\times$ more to classification than near-stationary ones, with 6/7 features exhibiting significant Granger causality (Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-causality}).
10	
11	The classifier is a proof-of-concept, not a deployable system: document-level gen-1 per-class F1\,=\,0.63 and ${\sim}30\%$ error rate are insufficient for automated filtering but sufficient for corpus-level flagging ($O(1/\sqrt{n})$ error scaling), with the primary difficulty being gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+ discrimination (86\% false negatives; Appendix~\ref{app:error-analysis}).
12	Practical utility lies in batch auditing (gen-2+ proportion thresholds) and provenance tracing (seed-lineage reconstruction).
13	In generator-unknown deployment, LOGO F1\,=\,0.694 provides a conservative lower bound (9.1\,pp per-generator spread); cross-temperature transfer degrades when test-time temperature exceeds training (F1\,$\to$\,0.723; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-temp}).
```

</details>

Where prior work documents \\emph{what converges}, DDR identifies \\emph{what is preserved}---enabling depth detection without per-generator calibration.\n\\item \\textbf{Task formulation and evaluation suite.} We define generation depth classification as an ordinal prediction task and construct a controlled dataset of 37K texts from eight generators across five families (0.6B--8B), with leave-one-generator-out cross-validation as the primary evaluation protocol.\n\\end{enumerate}\nWe additionally provide a proof-of-concept classifier (binary F1\\,=\\,0.694) and systematically map its operating boundaries across temperature, prompt type, instruction tuning, and model scale, establishing where DDR-based detection succeeds and where it fails.\n```\n**理由**：Claim 3 从 numbered contribution 降为叙述性补充。审稿人看 contributions list 时只看到 2 个核心贡献（DDR + task formulation），分类器是 \"additionally\" 提供的工具而非独立贡献。\n\n### B2. conclusion.tex（对应修改）\n**当前 L4-5** 隐含了三个贡献（DDR + classification + model collapse lens）。降级后无需大改，因为 Conclusion 本身没有 numbered list。当前措辞已经以 DDR 为主句、classification 为从句（\"This decomposition enables...\"），结构上与降级一致。**不需要改**。\n\n---\n\n## C. Cross-section consistency 检查清单\n\n修改后需要联动检查的所有位置：\n\n| 文件 | 行 | 当前内容 | 需要确认 |\n|------|-----|---------|---------|\n| abstract.tex | L6 | F1 数字 | 与 A1 修改一致 |\n| introduction.tex | L14 | F1 数字 | 与 A2 修改一致 |\n| introduction.tex | L17-21 | Contributions list | 与 B1 修改一致 |\n| results.tex | L17 | \"establishing the empirical decomposition---not the specific classifier---as DDR's primary contribution\" | 已与降级叙事一致，**无需改** ✓ |\n| discussion.tex | L11 | \"proof-of-concept, not a deployable system\" | 已与降级叙事一致，**无需改** ✓ |\n| discussion.tex | L20 | QLoRA gap 讨论 | 已与降级叙事一致，**无需改** ✓ |\n| conclusion.tex | L5 | F1 数字 | 与 A4 修改一致 |\n| Table 3 caption (results.tex L129) | | Baseline comparison | **无需改**（表格本身不受 headline 弱化影响） |\n\n**关键一致性规则**：修改后，Abstract / Intro / Conclusion 中出现的 F1 数字应统一为 {0.694 (LOGO), 0.905 (QLoRA)}，不再在摘要级别提 0.830 cross-model 数字。0.830 仅在 Results §5.1 L14 保留（有 footnote 注明 subset 差异）。\n\n---\n\n## 风险评估\n- 修改量小（4 处文本 + 1 处结构调整），cross-section 一致性风险低\n- 不涉及任何数字变动（所有 F1 值来自已有实验）\n- 叙事方向与 Discussion L11（proof-of-concept 定位）和 Results L17（decomposition 是 primary contribution）已有的措辞完全一致",

```text
1	% Abstract — Depth Fingerprinting of Recursive LLM Generation Chains
2	\begin{abstract}
3	When LLM outputs re-enter generation pipelines, each recursive step compresses \emph{where} text sits in distributional space while leaving \emph{how} it is shaped nearly unchanged.
4	We formalize this asymmetry as \emph{differential depth response} (DDR) and characterize it across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B parameters): under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.9$, $\tau \leq 0.7$), NLL location statistics and lexical diversity drift monotonically with generation depth (Cohen's $d{=}0.76$--$1.17$), while distributional shape features remain near-stationary ($d < 0.10$)---a separation validated by five independent effect-size metrics and data-driven clustering (silhouette\,=\,0.632).
5	Where prior work on iterative generation documents \emph{what converges}, the novel observation here is \emph{what is preserved}: shape invariance under the same process that compresses location provides the stable reference needed for depth estimation without per-generator calibration.
6	Exploiting DDR, a 7-feature ensemble achieves leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694 (95\% CI [.688, .700]) under leave-one-generator-out evaluation and 0.830 on heterogeneous cross-model chains, matching multi-detector ensembles at 2.4$\times$ lower inference cost.
7	The signal attenuates under instruction tuning; current applicability is restricted to base models.
8	\end{abstract}
9	
```

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	Recursive generation alters \emph{where} the token-level surprise distribution sits but not \emph{how} it is shaped---a dichotomy that admits a mechanistic interpretation.
5	Under nucleus sampling, top-$p$ truncation selects from a fixed probability-mass fraction at each step, preserving the functional form of the conditional distribution regardless of input content.
6	What changes across recursive steps is the \emph{input} to this truncation: as the model conditions on its own increasingly self-typical outputs, the expected token-level NLL compresses monotonically---each generation feeds slightly more predictable input into the same shape-preserving mechanism.
7	This account yields two testable predictions: (1)~increasing sampling randomness should weaken the location drift by disrupting mode-seeking, and (2)~semantic-preservation constraints should attenuate it by anchoring the input distribution.
8	Both are empirically confirmed: at $t{=}1.0$, the location signal collapses ($\rho$: $-0.60 \to -0.002$; Appendix~\ref{app:sampling-sensitivity}); under elaboration prompts, drift weakens relative to continuation (F1\,=\,0.730 vs.\ 0.781; Appendix~\ref{app:prompt-type}), consistent with \citet{broken_telephone}, while cross-type transfer remains viable (F1\,=\,0.753).
9	Permutation importance, Granger causality, and feature intervention analyses provide further causal evidence: drifting features contribute $5.5\times$ more to classification than near-stationary ones, with 6/7 features exhibiting significant Granger causality (Appendix~\ref{app:ddr-causality}).
10	
11	The classifier is a proof-of-concept, not a deployable system: document-level gen-1 per-class F1\,=\,0.63 and ${\sim}30\%$ error rate are insufficient for automated filtering but sufficient for corpus-level flagging ($O(1/\sqrt{n})$ error scaling), with the primary difficulty being gen-1 vs.\ gen-2+ discrimination (86\% false negatives; Appendix~\ref{app:error-analysis}).
12	Practical utility lies in batch auditing (gen-2+ proportion thresholds) and provenance tracing (seed-lineage reconstruction).
13	In generator-unknown deployment, LOGO F1\,=\,0.694 provides a conservative lower bound (9.1\,pp per-generator spread); cross-temperature transfer degrades when test-time temperature exceeds training (F1\,$\to$\,0.723; Appendix~\ref{app:cross-temp}).
14	
15	Preliminary evidence from a 14.7B generator (Qwen3-14B-Base) confirms that the DDR partition persists at larger scale: Cohen's $d$ for drifting features remains in the large-effect range ($d{=}0.78$--$1.12$), though Spearman $|\rho|$ attenuates by 21--35\%, with entropy mean falling just below the drifting threshold ($|\rho|{=}0.39 < 0.40$, $d{=}0.78$).
16	This scale-dependent signal compression is consistent with larger models producing more self-typical outputs that narrow inter-depth contrasts; the partition structure is preserved even as absolute effect sizes compress.
17	
18	DDR connects to but is mechanistically distinct from model collapse \cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}: retraining permanently erodes tail coverage by updating model weights, whereas inference-chain drift at fixed parameters shifts only the NLL distribution's location, leaving its shape as a stable reference.
19	Crucially, vocabulary impoverishment alone cannot explain DDR: if depth merely simplified the lexicon, shape features should also compress, yet entropy extremes and tail decay remain stable across all eight generators ($d < 0.10$; \S\ref{sec:results-differential}).
20	Moreover, semantic similarity between adjacent depths \emph{increases} monotonically (cosine: 0.62$\to$0.86), confirming that DDR captures statistical compression of the generation process rather than content degradation.
21	Prior work documents \emph{what converges}---diversity loss \cite{curious_decline}, attractor dynamics \cite{markovian_chains}, mode collapse \cite{curse_of_recursion}---but not what is \emph{preserved}; this complementary observation is what enables depth detection without per-generator calibration.
22	Three depth classes represent the practical resolution ceiling (5-class F1\,$\approx$\,0.50; Appendix~\ref{app:finer-depth}), though QLoRA recovers substantially more signal (F1\,=\,0.905; Appendix~\ref{app:qlora}); the $+$11.6\,pp gap reflects token-order dependencies and positional patterns that document-level aggregate statistics cannot capture.
23	Instruction-tuned models show attenuated fingerprints (OLMo-Instruct F1\,=\,0.338 vs.\ 0.763 base) with reversed feature correlations, suggesting alignment reshapes the generation attractor; QLoRA recovers to 0.89--0.91 on instruct data (Appendix~\ref{app:instruct}).
24	
```

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	AI text detection asks a single binary question---\emph{human or machine?}---and recent methods answer it with increasing reliability \cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,ld_score}.
5	Yet this framing elides a richer structure emerging on the open web: as LLM outputs re-enter training corpora \cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \emph{recursive depths}---an estimated 7--17\% of recent scientific publications already contain substantial LLM-modified content \cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}---and each generation cycle pushes distributional properties further from both the human source and the first-generation predecessor.
6	Knowing \emph{how many times} text has been recursively generated matters for data provenance \cite{ccnet,dsir} and model collapse analysis \cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}, yet no current method moves beyond the binary boundary.
7	
8	We introduce \emph{depth fingerprinting}: classifying text by ordinal generation depth (gen-0\,/\,gen-1\,/\,gen-2+) and decomposing the distributional drift that accumulates across depths into structurally distinct components.
9	The core empirical finding is a \emph{differential depth response} (DDR): across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B), the NLL distribution's central tendency (mean entropy, lexical diversity) drifts monotonically with depth while its shape (entropy extremes, tail structure) remains an order of magnitude more stable ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$ vs.\ $d < 0.10$; five independent effect-size metrics converge on this partition).
10	This location/shape asymmetry exposes a structural property of fixed-parameter autoregressive sampling: the decoding mechanism systematically compresses where the output distribution sits while preserving its higher-order form---a pattern mechanistically distinct from the tail-coverage erosion in model collapse \cite{curse_of_recursion}, and analytically informative independent of classification accuracy.
11	Concurrent work characterizes convergence in paraphrastic and translation chains \cite{broken_telephone,markovian_chains}; ours is the first to decompose the drift into complementary location and shape components and to leverage this asymmetry for cross-generator depth prediction.
12	We restrict to base (pretrained) models to isolate the generation dynamics from alignment artifacts (\S\ref{sec:discussion}).
13	
14	As a proof of concept, a lightweight 7-feature ensemble informed by DDR achieves leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694 under leave-one-generator-out evaluation and 0.830 on cross-model chains---demonstrating that the DDR decomposition carries practical predictive power, though the primary contribution is the structural analysis rather than classification performance per se.
15	
16	Our contributions:
17	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
18	    \item \textbf{Empirical decomposition (DDR).} Data-driven clustering ($k{=}2$, silhouette\,=\,0.632) reveals that inference-chain drift separates into a drifting location component ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) and a stable shape component ($d < 0.10$). Where prior work documents \emph{what converges}, DDR identifies \emph{what is preserved}---enabling depth detection without per-generator calibration.
19	    \item \textbf{Task formulation and evaluation suite.} We define generation depth classification as an ordinal prediction task and construct a controlled dataset of 37K texts from eight generators across five families (0.6B--8B), with leave-one-generator-out cross-validation as the primary evaluation protocol.
20	    \item \textbf{Boundary analysis.} We systematically map the operating boundaries of DDR-based depth detection across temperature, prompt type, instruction tuning, and model scale, identifying where the signal persists and where it breaks down.
21	\end{enumerate}
22	
```

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	Recursive generation leaves a systematic distributional signature: central-tendency features drift monotonically with depth while shape features remain an order of magnitude more stable---a dichotomy we formalize as differential depth response (DDR), validated by five independent effect-size metrics across eight generators from five model families.
5	This decomposition enables cross-generator depth classification (leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694 under LOGO, 0.830 on cross-model chains) and provides an analytical lens complementary to model collapse: fixed-parameter sampling compresses distributional location while preserving shape, whereas retraining erodes both.
6	Current boundaries are instruction tuning---where alignment reshapes the generation attractor---and frontier scale, where preliminary 14B evidence confirms the DDR partition with attenuated effect sizes; 70B+ models remain untested.
7	
```

```text
106	\subsection{Comparison with Detection Baselines}
107	\label{sec:results-baselines}
108	
109	Existing detection methods target binary real-vs-synthetic classification; their continuous scores encode distributional divergence that may incidentally correlate with recursive depth.
110	To test this, we repurpose four detection scores as ordinal depth predictors under identical XGBoost and LOGO conditions (Table~\ref{tab:baselines}).
111	
112	\begin{table}[t]
113	\centering
114	\footnotesize
115	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
116	\toprule
117	\textbf{Method} & \textbf{Feat.} & \textbf{3-cl F1} & \textbf{Bin F1} & \textbf{ms/text} \\
118	\midrule
119	4-det.\ + surface     & 6 & \textbf{0.795} & \textbf{0.708} & 22.8 \\
120	4-detector ens.\      & 4 & 0.785 & 0.700 & 22.8 \\
121	7-feature (ours)      & 7 & 0.774 & 0.705 & \textbf{9.7} \\
122	\midrule
123	Avg-LogProb           & 1 & 0.707 & 0.673 & --- \\
124	FastDetectGPT         & 1 & 0.660 & 0.569 & --- \\
125	LD-Score              & 1 & 0.630 & 0.675 & --- \\
126	Binoculars            & 1 & 0.411 & 0.539 & --- \\
127	\bottomrule
128	\end{tabular}
129	\caption{Depth classification (8-gen LOGO, pooled evaluation; slightly higher than per-generator macro average in Table~\ref{tab:logo-results}). Identical XGBoost configuration. 7-feature: one 410M model (905\,MB); 4-det ensembles: two models, 1.4B params (2{,}868\,MB). Individual detection scores (N/A) share the reference-model forward pass; per-method timing is not separately profiled.}
130	\label{tab:baselines}
131	\end{table}
132	
133	\begin{figure}[t]
134	\centering
135	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{fig3_baseline_comparison.pdf}
136	\caption{LOGO binary F1 vs.\ inference cost (8-gen pooled). The 7-feature ensemble matches multi-detector ensembles at 2.4$\times$ lower cost; individual detectors vary widely when repurposed for depth.}
137	\label{fig:baseline-ci}
138	\end{figure}
139	
140	The 7-feature ensemble surpasses all individual baselines in 3-class F1 ($\Delta$\,=\,6.7--36.3\,pp; Figure~\ref{fig:baseline-ci}) and the pure 4-detector ensemble in binary F1 (0.705 vs.\ 0.700).
141	Adding DDR's surface features to the four detectors yields the highest binary F1 (0.708), marginally above the 7-feature set at 2.4$\times$ the inference cost.
142	This result underscores that DDR's primary contribution is not classification performance---where multi-detector ensembles match or marginally exceed it---but the decomposition of depth signal into location-sensitive and shape-stable components, a structural distinction absent from both binary detection methods and model collapse analyses (\S\ref{sec:discussion}).
143	
```

```text
1	% Abstract — Depth Fingerprinting of Recursive LLM Generation Chains
2	\begin{abstract}
3	When LLM outputs re-enter generation pipelines, each recursive step compresses \emph{where} text sits in distributional space while leaving \emph{how} it is shaped nearly unchanged.
4	We formalize this asymmetry as \emph{differential depth response} (DDR) and characterize it across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B parameters): under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.9$, $\tau \leq 0.7$), NLL location statistics and lexical diversity drift monotonically with generation depth (Cohen's $d{=}0.76$--$1.17$), while distributional shape features remain near-stationary ($d < 0.10$)---a separation validated by five independent effect-size metrics and data-driven clustering (silhouette\,=\,0.632).
5	Where prior work on iterative generation documents \emph{what converges}, the novel observation here is \emph{what is preserved}: shape invariance under the same process that compresses location provides the stable reference needed for depth estimation without per-generator calibration.
6	As a proof of concept, a lightweight 7-feature ensemble exploiting DDR achieves leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694 under leave-one-generator-out evaluation, confirming that the decomposition yields actionable depth signal; an end-to-end classifier recovers substantially more (F1\,=\,0.905), establishing the analytical framework---not the specific features---as the primary contribution.
7	The signal attenuates under instruction tuning; current applicability is restricted to base models.
8	\end{abstract}
9	
```

1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	Recursive generation leaves a systematic distributional signature: central-tendency features drift monotonically with depth while shape features remain an order of magnitude more stable---a dichotomy we formalize as differential depth response (DDR), validated by five independent effect-size metrics across eight generators from five model families.
5	This decomposition enables cross-generator depth classification (leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694 under LOGO; end-to-end: 0.905) and provides an analytical lens complementary to model collapse: fixed-parameter sampling compresses distributional location while preserving shape, whereas retraining erodes both.
6	Current boundaries are instruction tuning---where alignment reshapes the generation attractor---and frontier scale, where preliminary 14B evidence confirms the DDR partition with attenuated effect sizes; 70B+ models remain untested.
7

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	AI text detection asks a single binary question---\emph{human or machine?}---and recent methods answer it with increasing reliability \cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,ld_score}.
5	Yet this framing elides a richer structure emerging on the open web: as LLM outputs re-enter training corpora \cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \emph{recursive depths}---an estimated 7--17\% of recent scientific publications already contain substantial LLM-modified content \cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}---and each generation cycle pushes distributional properties further from both the human source and the first-generation predecessor.
6	Knowing \emph{how many times} text has been recursively generated matters for data provenance \cite{ccnet,dsir} and model collapse analysis \cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}, yet no current method moves beyond the binary boundary.
7	
8	We introduce \emph{depth fingerprinting}: classifying text by ordinal generation depth (gen-0\,/\,gen-1\,/\,gen-2+) and decomposing the distributional drift that accumulates across depths into structurally distinct components.
9	The core empirical finding is a \emph{differential depth response} (DDR): across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B), the NLL distribution's central tendency (mean entropy, lexical diversity) drifts monotonically with depth while its shape (entropy extremes, tail structure) remains an order of magnitude more stable ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$ vs.\ $d < 0.10$; five independent effect-size metrics converge on this partition).
10	This location/shape asymmetry exposes a structural property of fixed-parameter autoregressive sampling: the decoding mechanism systematically compresses where the output distribution sits while preserving its higher-order form---a pattern mechanistically distinct from the tail-coverage erosion in model collapse \cite{curse_of_recursion}, and analytically informative independent of classification accuracy.
11	Concurrent work characterizes convergence in paraphrastic and translation chains \cite{broken_telephone,markovian_chains}; ours is the first to decompose the drift into complementary location and shape components and to leverage this asymmetry for cross-generator depth prediction.
12	We restrict to base (pretrained) models to isolate the generation dynamics from alignment artifacts (\S\ref{sec:discussion}).
13	
14	As a proof of concept, a lightweight 7-feature ensemble informed by DDR achieves leak-free binary F1\,=\,0.694 under leave-one-generator-out evaluation, surpassing individual detection baselines by 6.7--36.3\,pp; an end-to-end QLoRA classifier reaches 0.905, confirming that the depth signal extends well beyond handcrafted features.
15	
16	Our contributions:
17	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
18	    \item \textbf{Empirical decomposition (DDR).} Data-driven clustering ($k{=}2$, silhouette\,=\,0.632) reveals that inference-chain drift separates into a drifting location component ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) and a stable shape component ($d < 0.10$). Where prior work documents \emph{what converges}, DDR identifies \emph{what is preserved}---enabling depth detection without per-generator calibration.
19	    \item \textbf{Task formulation and evaluation suite.} We define generation depth classification as an ordinal prediction task and construct a controlled dataset of 37K texts from eight generators across five families (0.6B--8B), with leave-one-generator-out cross-validation as the primary evaluation protocol.
20	\end{enumerate}
21	We additionally provide a proof-of-concept classifier (binary F1\,=\,0.694) and systematically map its operating boundaries across temperature, prompt type, instruction tuning, and model scale, establishing where DDR-based detection succeeds and where it fails.
22	
```

      "content": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nAI text detection asks a single binary question---\\emph{human or machine?}---and recent methods answer it with increasing reliability \\cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,ld_score}.\nYet this framing elides a richer structure emerging on the open web: as LLM outputs re-enter training corpora \\cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \\emph{recursive depths}---an estimated 7--17\\% of recent scientific publications already contain substantial LLM-modified content \\cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}---and each generation cycle pushes distributional properties further from both the human source and the first-generation predecessor.\nKnowing \\emph{how many times} text has been recursively generated matters for data provenance \\cite{ccnet,dsir} and model collapse analysis \\cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}, yet no current method moves beyond the binary boundary.\n\nWe introduce \\emph{depth fingerprinting}: classifying text by ordinal generation depth (gen-0\\,/\\,gen-1\\,/\\,gen-2+) and decomposing the distributional drift that accumulates across depths into structurally distinct components.\nThe core empirical finding is a \\emph{differential depth response} (DDR): across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B), the NLL distribution's central tendency (mean entropy, lexical diversity) drifts monotonically with depth while its shape (entropy extremes, tail structure) remains an order of magnitude more stable ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$ vs.\\ $d < 0.10$; five independent effect-size metrics converge on this partition).\nThis location/shape asymmetry exposes a structural property of fixed-parameter autoregressive sampling: the decoding mechanism systematically compresses where the output distribution sits while preserving its higher-order form---a pattern mechanistically distinct from the tail-coverage erosion in model collapse \\cite{curse_of_recursion}, and analytically informative independent of classification accuracy.\nConcurrent work characterizes convergence in paraphrastic and translation chains \\cite{broken_telephone,markovian_chains}; ours is the first to decompose the drift into complementary location and shape components and to leverage this asymmetry for cross-generator depth prediction.\nWe restrict to base (pretrained) models to isolate the generation dynamics from alignment artifacts (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).\n\nAs a proof of concept, a lightweight 7-feature ensemble informed by DDR achieves leak-free binary F1\\,=\\,0.694 under leave-one-generator-out evaluation, surpassing individual detection baselines by 6.7--36.3\\,pp; an end-to-end QLoRA classifier reaches 0.905, confirming that the depth signal extends well beyond handcrafted features.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n    \\item \\textbf{Empirical decomposition (DDR).} Data-driven clustering ($k{=}2$, silhouette\\,=\\,0.632) reveals that inference-chain drift separates into a drifting location component ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) and a stable shape component ($d < 0.10$). Where prior work documents \\emph{what converges}, DDR identifies \\emph{what is preserved}---enabling depth detection without per-generator calibration.\n    \\item \\textbf{Task formulation and evaluation suite.} We define generation depth classification as an ordinal prediction task and construct a controlled dataset of 37K texts from eight generators across five families (0.6B--8B), with leave-one-generator-out cross-validation as the primary evaluation protocol.\n\\end{enumerate}\nWe additionally provide a proof-of-concept classifier (binary F1\\,=\\,0.694) and systematically map its operating boundaries across temperature, prompt type, instruction tuning, and model scale, establishing where DDR-based detection succeeds and where it fails.\n",
      "numLines": 22,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 22
    }
  },

[System 触发审查]:
**请求：整体 story 说服力评估（审稿人视角）**

Framing 刚做了重大调整（分类 headline 弱化、Claim 3 降级、contributions 3→2），cross-section 数字一致性已通过。现在需要你从 EMNLP Findings 审稿人视角做一次整体评估：

**核心问题：DDR 作为 primary contribution 够不够撑住整篇？**

请按以下维度评估（简短打分 + 一句话理由）：

1. **Novelty**：DDR decomposition（location drift vs shape stability）在 model collapse / iterative generation 文献中是否足够新颖？"what is preserved" 这个角度有没有说服力？

2. **Technical depth**：5 independent effect-size metrics + clustering + TOST + causality analysis，作为 empirical paper 的方法论深度够不够？

3. **Scope**：8 generators / 5 families / 4 domains / 3 prompt types / 4 temperatures + robustness + instruct + scale — 对 EMNLP Findings 够不够？

4. **Weakness exposure**：新 framing 下最大的 3 个弱点是什么？审稿人最可能用什么理由给 2.5-3.0？

5. **一句话判断**：以当前 story + 数据，EMNLP Findings 3.5 是 likely / possible / unlikely？

不需要建议修改方案，只要诚实评估。

```json
{
  "text": "[severity: info] 整体 story 说服力评估（EMNLP Findings 审稿人视角）\n\n## 1. Novelty — 3/5（中等）\n\n\"What is preserved\" 角度在 iterative generation 文献中确实是新的——broken_telephone 和 markovian_chains 都聚焦 convergence/information loss，没有人做过 location vs shape 的分离。但 location drift 本身（entropy mean 下降、vocabulary 贫化）在 model collapse 文献中是常识。DDR 的创新在于 shape stability 的发现和形式化，这是一个有价值但幅度有限的增量贡献。审稿人会说：\"interesting observation, but is formalizing a partition enough for a paper?\"\n\n## 2. Technical depth — 4/5（扎实）\n\n5 个 effect-size metrics（Cohen's d, Glass's Δ, Cliff's δ, KS, CV shift）互相印证，k=2 clustering with silhouette，TOST equivalence testing with bootstrap，Granger causality + permutation importance + feature intervention。对 empirical paper 而言方法论严谨度超过平均水平。ε sensitivity analysis 和 holdout split validation 也是加分项。审稿人很难攻击统计方法论。\n\n## 3. Scope — 4/5（充分）\n\n8 generators / 5 families / 0.6B-14B / 4 domains / 3 prompt types / 4 temperatures / 3 reference models / seed independence / length confound / human edit robustness / QLoRA end-to-end。对 EMNLP Findings 绰绰有余。唯一的 scope gap 是 base-model-only，但这已被诚实标注为 limitation 且有初步 instruct 数据。\n\n## 4. 最大 3 个弱点（审稿人给 2.5-3.0 的理由）\n\n**W1. \"So what?\" — DDR 的 downstream impact 不清晰。** DDR 分解很漂亮，但论文没有回答\"知道 location drifts / shape preserves 之后能做什么新的事情？\"分类器是 proof-of-concept 但 F1=0.694 不够 deploy；downstream utility 实验是 negative（已降级）。审稿人会问：DDR 除了作为一个 observation，对 NLP 社区有什么 actionable insight？当前论文的回答是\"data provenance + model collapse analysis\"，但都停在概念层面没有实证 demo。**这是最可能导致 3.0 而非 3.5 的理由。**\n\n**W2. Base-model-only scope 在 2026 年缺乏实际价值。** Web 上绝大多数 LLM 生成文本来自 instruct/RLHF 模型。OLMo-Instruct F1=0.338 说明 DDR 在实际场景下基本失效。论文已诚实披露，但审稿人可以合理质疑：一个只对 base model 有效的 decomposition，对 NLP 社区有多大价值？QLoRA 在 instruct 上恢复到 0.89-0.91 部分缓解了这一点，但这也暗示 DDR 的 handcrafted features 本身不够。\n\n**W3. Novelty 的边界感——DDR 是 observation 还是 contribution？** 将一个 empirical observation（location drifts, shape doesn't）包装为 \"differential depth response\" 并做详尽验证，这在 EMNLP 审稿标准下是否构成足够的贡献？部分审稿人会认为这更适合 workshop paper 或作为更大工作的一部分，而非独立 Findings paper。\n\n## 5. 一句话判断\n\n**EMNLP Findings 3.5：possible but not likely。**\n\nDream median 3.0、best 3.25 是更现实的基准。Framing 改善（proof-of-concept 定位、shape ≠ vocab 论据、cross-section 一致性）可能将部分审稿人从 3.0 推到 3.5，但 W1（\"so what\"）和 W2（base-model scope）是结构性弱点，framing 无法完全弥补。最可能的结果是 median 3.0-3.25，individual reviewers 在 2.5-3.5 之间分布。能否拿到 Findings 取决于是否碰到认可 empirical decomposition 本身价值的审稿人。",
  "project": "gen-depth-fingerprint"
}

  "content": "[System 触发审查]:\n**请求：W1/W2 可行动项穷举评估（需签字结论）**\n\n你上轮给了 Novelty 3/5 和两个结构性弱点。我不应该直接认命。请逐条评估是否有实验或分析能缩小 gap：\n\n## W1 \"So what\" — DDR 的 downstream impact\n\n**候选行动**（逐个评估可行性 + 边际收益）：\n- A. 用现有 39 个实验数据做一个 **corpus-level contamination detection demo**：给定一个 corpus，用 DDR 特征估算 gen-2+ 占比，设一个 threshold，看能不能从 mixed corpus 中 flag 高递归深度的 batch。不需要新 GPU 实验，纯分析。\n- B. 在 Discussion 中加一个 **concrete application scenario**（比如 training data audit pipeline），用我们的数据给出可操作的 threshold（\"if mean entropy < X and TTR < Y, flag as likely gen-2+\"）\n- C. 把 DDR 的 shape stability 连接到 **watermark preservation**：shape 不漂移意味着 watermark 特征在递归中可能保持——这是一个有 novelty 的推测，可以在 Discussion 中写 2-3 句作为 future work\n\n## W2 Base-model scope\n\n**候选行动**：\n- D. **用 1 卡跑 1 个 instruct 模型的 DDR 恢复实验**：我们已有 OLMo-Instruct (F1=0.338) 和 Qwen3-Instruct 数据。能否对 instruct 数据用不同的 feature set（比如加 perplexity ratio 或其他 instruct-aware features）做 partial recovery？这可以把 W2 从\"completely fails\"变成\"partially recoverable with adapted features\"\n- E. **在 Limitations/Discussion 中加入更强的 instruct signal 分析**：用现有 instruct 数据分析 WHY DDR 失效（哪些特征反转、哪些保持），让审稿人看到我们理解了 failure mode 而不是简单放弃\n- F. **跑一个 chat-format instruct chain**：用 Qwen3-1.7B-Instruct 做 multi-turn chat 形式的递归生成，看 DDR 在 chat context 下是否有 partial signal。这是一个新实验维度。\n\n## 要求\n\n对每个候选：\n1. 可行性（GPU 时间 / 分析时间 / DDL 风险）\n2. 边际收益（能把 3.0 推到多少？）\n3. 是否推荐\n\n最后给一个 **签字结论**：\"以下 N 个行动值得做 / 以下行动不值得做 / GPU 应该用于 X 或释放\"。"

## W1 "So what" — DDR 的 downstream impact

**候选行动**（逐个评估可行性 + 边际收益）：
- A. 用现有 39 个实验数据做一个 **corpus-level contamination detection demo**：给定一个 corpus，用 DDR 特征估算 gen-2+ 占比，设一个 threshold，看能不能从 mixed corpus 中 flag 高递归深度的 batch。不需要新 GPU 实验，纯分析。
- B. 在 Discussion 中加一个 **concrete application scenario**（比如 training data audit pipeline），用我们的数据给出可操作的 threshold（"if mean entropy < X and TTR < Y, flag as likely gen-2+"）
- C. 把 DDR 的 shape stability 连接到 **watermark preservation**：shape 不漂移意味着 watermark 特征在递归中可能保持——这是一个有 novelty 的推测，可以在 Discussion 中写 2-3 句作为 future work

## W2 Base-model scope

**候选行动**：
- D. **用 1 卡跑 1 个 instruct 模型的 DDR 恢复实验**：我们已有 OLMo-Instruct (F1=0.338) 和 Qwen3-Instruct 数据。能否对 instruct 数据用不同的 feature set（比如加 perplexity ratio 或其他 instruct-aware features）做 partial recovery？这可以把 W2 从"completely fails"变成"partially recoverable with adapted features"
- E. **在 Limitations/Discussion 中加入更强的 instruct signal 分析**：用现有 instruct 数据分析 WHY DDR 失效（哪些特征反转、哪些保持），让审稿人看到我们理解了 failure mode 而不是简单放弃
- F. **跑一个 chat-format instruct chain**：用 Qwen3-1.7B-Instruct 做 multi-turn chat 形式的递归生成，看 DDR 在 chat context 下是否有 partial signal。这是一个新实验维度。

## 要求

对每个候选：
1. 可行性（GPU 时间 / 分析时间 / DDL 风险）
2. 边际收益（能把 3.0 推到多少？）
3. 是否推荐

最后给一个 **签字结论**："以下 N 个行动值得做 / 以下行动不值得做 / GPU 应该用于 X 或释放"。

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am an independent scientific reviewer (Reviewer role) for the `gen-depth-fingerprint` project targeting EMNLP 2026 ARR (DDL 2026-05-25). My role is to audit worker outputs, verify experimental claims, check cross-section consistency, and report findings to Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. This session covered:
   - Completing the R34 Dream action matrix review (5 specific questions)
   - ROADMAP update reviews (D032, D033, v35 delivery)
   - NLL distribution figure verification (generator set mismatch → fix → final verification)
   - Bibliography expansion and 3 new citations review
   - Providing specific Claim 3 downgrade + classification headline weakening plan
   - Cross-section consistency checks (2 rounds: initial issues found → fixes verified)
   - Overall story persuasiveness assessment (Novelty 3/5, Findings 3.5 possible but not likely)
   - **Currently in progress**: Evaluating 6 candidate actions to address W1 ("so what") and W2 (base-model scope)

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **DDR (Differential Depth Response)**: Core finding — location features drift (|ρ|>0.60, d=0.76-1.17), shape features stable (d<0.10). k=2 clustering, silhouette=0.632
   - **LOGO (Leave-One-Generator-Out)**: Primary evaluation. Binary F1=0.694 (leak-free, gen-1 vs gen-2+)
   - **QLoRA**: End-to-end classifier F1=0.905 (3-class, 6-gen LOGO). +11.6pp over 7-feature
   - **Framing shift**: Classification downgraded to proof-of-concept; DDR decomposition is primary contribution. Contributions 3→2 numbered + 1 narrative
   - **F1 number policy**: Abstract/Intro/Conclusion use {0.694, 0.905} only. 0.830 (cross-model, 5-gen subset) retained only in Results §5.1 and appendix
   - **Dream R29 scores**: best 3.25, median 3.0, worst 2.5. Internal R34: 3.5
   - **exp_060**: Qwen3-14B-Base DDR — pattern confirmed but entropy_mean |ρ|=0.39 below 0.4 threshold
   - **Instruct models**: OLMo-Instruct F1=0.338 (base=0.763); QLoRA recovers to 0.89-0.91; entropy_mean ρ reverses sign; DDR silhouette drops 0.632→0.338
   - **Server**: bjb2-18868, 4× RTX 5090 32GB, GPU whitelist cuda:0-3, 4 cards idle

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`artifacts/_project/paper/abstract.tex`**
     - Updated L6: "As a proof of concept, a lightweight 7-feature ensemble exploiting DDR achieves leak-free binary F1=0.694... an end-to-end classifier recovers substantially more (F1=0.905), establishing the analytical framework—not the specific features—as the primary contribution."
     - Removed 0.830 and 2.4× cost comparison from headline
   
   - **`artifacts/_project/paper/introduction.tex`**
     - L14 updated: "surpassing individual detection baselines by 6.7–36.3 pp; an end-to-end QLoRA classifier reaches 0.905"
     - L17-21: Contributions restructured to 2 numbered (DDR + Task formulation) + 1 narrative paragraph ("We additionally provide a proof-of-concept classifier...")
     - L20 new Claim 3: "Boundary analysis. We systematically map the operating boundaries..."
   
   - **`artifacts/_project/paper/conclusion.tex`**
     - L5: "enables cross-generator depth classification (leak-free binary F1=0.694 under LOGO; end-to-end: 0.905)"
     - Replaced 0.830 with QLoRA 0.905

        "content": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nRecursive generation leaves a systematic distributional signature: central-tendency features drift monotonically with depth while shape features remain an order of magnitude more stable---a dichotomy we formalize as differential depth response (DDR), validated by five independent effect-size metrics across eight generators from five model families.\nThis decomposition enables cross-generator depth classification (leak-free binary F1\\,=\\,0.694 under LOGO; end-to-end: 0.905) and provides an analytical lens complementary to model collapse: fixed-parameter sampling compresses distributional location while preserving shape, whereas retraining erodes both.\nCurrent boundaries are instruction tuning---where alignment reshapes the generation attractor---and frontier scale, where preliminary 14B evidence confirms the DDR partition with attenuated effect sizes; 70B+ models remain untested.\n",
        "numLines": 7,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 7

    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/artifacts/_project/paper/introduction.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nAI text detection asks a single binary question---\\emph{human or machine?}---and recent methods answer it with increasing reliability \\cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,ld_score}.\nYet this framing elides a richer structure emerging on the open web: as LLM outputs re-enter training corpora \\cite{dolma,fineweb} and seed further generation, text accumulates at varying \\emph{recursive depths}---an estimated 7--17\\% of recent scientific publications already contain substantial LLM-modified content \\cite{mapping_llm_science,monitoring_ai_review}---and each generation cycle pushes distributional properties further from both the human source and the first-generation predecessor.\nKnowing \\emph{how many times} text has been recursively generated matters for data provenance \\cite{ccnet,dsir} and model collapse analysis \\cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}, yet no current method moves beyond the binary boundary.\n\nWe introduce \\emph{depth fingerprinting}: classifying text by ordinal generation depth (gen-0\\,/\\,gen-1\\,/\\,gen-2+) and decomposing the distributional drift that accumulates across depths into structurally distinct components.\nThe core empirical finding is a \\emph{differential depth response} (DDR): across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B), the NLL distribution's central tendency (mean entropy, lexical diversity) drifts monotonically with depth while its shape (entropy extremes, tail structure) remains an order of magnitude more stable ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$ vs.\\ $d < 0.10$; five independent effect-size metrics converge on this partition).\nThis location/shape asymmetry exposes a structural property of fixed-parameter autoregressive sampling: the decoding mechanism systematically compresses where the output distribution sits while preserving its higher-order form---a pattern mechanistically distinct from the tail-coverage erosion in model collapse \\cite{curse_of_recursion}, and analytically informative independent of classification accuracy.\nConcurrent work characterizes convergence in paraphrastic and translation chains \\cite{broken_telephone,markovian_chains}; ours is the first to decompose the drift into complementary location and shape components and to leverage this asymmetry for cross-generator depth prediction.\nWe restrict to base (pretrained) models to isolate the generation dynamics from alignment artifacts (\\S\\ref{sec:discussion}).\n\nAs a proof of concept, a lightweight 7-feature ensemble informed by DDR achieves leak-free binary F1\\,=\\,0.694 under leave-one-generator-out evaluation, surpassing individual detection baselines by 6.7--36.3\\,pp; an end-to-end QLoRA classifier reaches 0.905, confirming that the depth signal extends well beyond handcrafted features.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n    \\item \\textbf{Empirical decomposition (DDR).} Data-driven clustering ($k{=}2$, silhouette\\,=\\,0.632) reveals that inference-chain drift separates into a drifting location component ($d{=}0.76$--$1.17$) and a stable shape component ($d < 0.10$). Where prior work documents \\emph{what converges}, DDR identifies \\emph{what is preserved}---enabling depth detection without per-generator calibration.\n    \\item \\textbf{Task formulation and evaluation suite.} We define generation depth classification as an ordinal prediction task and construct a controlled dataset of 37K texts from eight generators across five families (0.6B--8B), with leave-one-generator-out cross-validation as the primary evaluation protocol.\n\\end{enumerate}\nWe additionally provide a proof-of-concept classifier (binary F1\\,=\\,0.694) and systematically map its operating boundaries across temperature, prompt type, instruction tuning, and model scale, establishing where DDR-based detection succeeds and where it fails.\n",
        "numLines": 22,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 22
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../artifacts/_project/paper/introduction.tex"

        "content": "% Abstract — Depth Fingerprinting of Recursive LLM Generation Chains\n\\begin{abstract}\nWhen LLM outputs re-enter generation pipelines, each recursive step compresses \\emph{where} text sits in distributional space while leaving \\emph{how} it is shaped nearly unchanged.\nWe formalize this asymmetry as \\emph{differential depth response} (DDR) and characterize it across eight base-model generators spanning five architectural families (0.6B--8B parameters): under nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.9$, $\\tau \\leq 0.7$), NLL location statistics and lexical diversity drift monotonically with generation depth (Cohen's $d{=}0.76$--$1.17$), while distributional shape features remain near-stationary ($d < 0.10$)---a separation validated by five independent effect-size metrics and data-driven clustering (silhouette\\,=\\,0.632).\nWhere prior work on iterative generation documents \\emph{what converges}, the novel observation here is \\emph{what is preserved}: shape invariance under the same process that compresses location provides the stable reference needed for depth estimation without per-generator calibration.\nAs a proof of concept, a lightweight 7-feature ensemble exploiting DDR achieves leak-free binary F1\\,=\\,0.694 under leave-one-generator-out evaluation, confirming that the decomposition yields actionable depth signal; an end-to-end classifier recovers substantially more (F1\\,=\\,0.905), establishing the analytical framework---not the specific features---as the primary contribution.\nThe signal attenuates under instruction tuning; current applicability is restricted to base models.\n\\end{abstract}\n",
        "numLines": 9,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 9
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../artifacts/_project/paper/abstract.tex"

    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/artifacts/_project/paper/discussion.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/artifacts/_project/paper/discussion.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\nRecursive generation alters \\emph{where} the token-level surprise distribution sits but not \\emph{how} it is shaped---a dichotomy that admits a mechanistic interpretation.\nUnder nucleus sampling, top-$p$ truncation selects from a fixed probability-mass fraction at each step, preserving the functional form of the conditional distribution regardless of input content.\nWhat changes across recursive steps is the \\emph{input} to this truncation: as the model conditions on its own increasingly self-typical outputs, the expected token-level NLL compresses monotonically---each generation feeds slightly more predictable input into the same shape-preserving mechanism.\nThis account yields two testable predictions: (1)~increasing sampling randomness should weaken the location drift by disrupting mode-seeking, and (2)~semantic-preservation constraints should attenuate it by anchoring the input distribution.\nBoth are empirically confirmed: at $t{=}1.0$, the location signal collapses ($\\rho$: $-0.60 \\to -0.002$; Appendix~\\ref{app:sampling-sensitivity}); under elaboration prompts, drift weakens relative to continuation (F1\\,=\\,0.730 vs.\\ 0.781; Appendix~\\ref{app:prompt-type}), consistent with \\citet{broken_telephone}, while cross-type transfer remains viable (F1\\,=\\,0.753).\nPermutation importance, Granger causality, and feature intervention analyses provide further causal evidence: drifting features contribute $5.5\\times$ more to classification than near-stationary ones, with 6/7 features exhibiting significant Granger causality (Appendix~\\ref{app:ddr-causality}).\n\nThe classifier is a proof-of-concept, not a deployable system: document-level gen-1 per-class F1\\,=\\,0.63 and ${\\sim}30\\%$ error rate are insufficient for automated filtering but sufficient for corpus-level flagging ($O(1/\\sqrt{n})$ error scaling), with the primary difficulty being gen-1 vs.\\ gen-2+ discrimination (86\\% false negatives; Appendix~\\ref{app:error-analysis}).\nPractical utility lies in batch auditing (gen-2+ proportion thresholds) and provenance tracing (seed-lineage reconstruction).\nIn generator-unknown deployment, LOGO F1\\,=\\,0.694 provides a conservative lower bound (9.1\\,pp per-generator spread); cross-temperature transfer degrades when test-time temperature exceeds training (F1\\,$\\to$\\,0.723; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-temp}).\n\nPreliminary evidence from a 14.7B generator (Qwen3-14B-Base) confirms that the DDR partition persists at larger scale: Cohen's $d$ for drifting features remains in the large-effect range ($d{=}0.78$--$1.12$), though Spearman $|\\rho|$ attenuates by 21--35\\%, with entropy mean falling just below the drifting threshold ($|\\rho|{=}0.39 < 0.40$, $d{=}0.78$).\nThis scale-dependent signal compression is consistent with larger models producing more self-typical outputs that narrow inter-depth contrasts; the partition structure is preserved even as absolute effect sizes compress.\n\nDDR connects to but is mechanistically distinct from model collapse \\cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse}: retraining permanently erodes tail coverage by updating model weights, whereas inference-chain drift at fixed parameters shifts only the NLL distribution's location, leaving its shape as a stable reference.\nCrucially, vocabulary impoverishment alone cannot explain DDR: if depth merely simplified the lexicon, shape features should also compress, yet entropy extremes and tail decay remain stable across all eight generators ($d < 0.10$; \\S\\ref{sec:results-differential}).\nMoreover, semantic similarity between adjacent depths \\emph{increases} monotonically (cosine: 0.62$\\to$0.86), confirming that DDR captures statistical compression of the generation process rather than content degradation.\nPrior work documents \\emph{what converges}---diversity loss \\cite{curious_decline}, attractor dynamics \\cite{markovian_chains}, mode collapse \\cite{curse_of_recursion}---but not what is \\emph{preserved}; this complementary observation is what enables depth detection without per-generator calibration.\nThree depth classes represent the practical resolution ceiling (5-class F1\\,$\\approx$\\,0.50; Appendix~\\ref{app:finer-depth}), though QLoRA recovers substantially more signal (F1\\,=\\,0.905; Appendix~\\ref{app:qlora}); the $+$11.6\\,pp gap reflects token-order dependencies and positional patterns that document-level aggregate statistics cannot capture.\nInstruction-tuned models show attenuated fingerprints (OLMo-Instruct F1\\,=\\,0.338 vs.\\ 0.763 base) with reversed feature correlations, suggesting alignment reshapes the generation attractor; QLoRA recovers to 0.89--0.91 on instruct data (Appendix~\\ref{app:instruct}).\n",
        "numLines": 24,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 24
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../artifacts/_project/paper/discussion.tex"

        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/artifacts/_project/paper/related_work.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related}\n\n\\subsection{AI-Generated Text Detection}\n\\label{sec:related-detection}\n\nThe detection literature spans statistical tests \\cite{gltr,grover,release_strategies}, zero-shot scoring \\cite{detectgpt,fast_detectgpt,binoculars,dna_gpt}, and learned classifiers \\cite{ghostbuster,feat_aug_transformer,reliable_detection,ld_score}; see \\citet{wu_survey_2025} and \\citet{jawahar_survey} for surveys.\nRAID \\cite{raid} benchmarks binary detection across generators and attacks; we extend the task from binary to ordinal depth estimation.\nSeveral recent efforts move beyond the binary boundary---collaboration roles \\cite{beyond_binary}, joint detection-attribution \\cite{two_birds}, creator-editor modes \\cite{beyond_final_actor}, multi-generator attribution \\cite{semeval2024_task8}---but all taxonomize by \\emph{source} or \\emph{production mode}, not recursive generation \\emph{depth}; repurposing their scores as depth predictors reveals high cross-generator variance (binary F1\\,=\\,0.52--0.71; Table~\\ref{tab:baselines}) \\cite{paraphrase_evades,detection_possibilities}.\nOur contribution is orthogonal: the task formulation itself (ordinal depth classification \\cite{gutierrez_ordinal_survey,coral_ordinal,kasa_ordinality_nlp}) and the finding that drifting and stationary features together yield consistent cross-generator performance where individual detection scores do not \\cite{uchendu_attribution,munir_looking_glass}.\n\n\\subsection{Model Collapse and Iterative Generation}\n\\label{sec:related-collapse}\n\nSelf-consuming generative models progressively lose distributional coverage \\cite{self_consuming_mad,curse_of_recursion,seddik_synthetic_data}, though data mixing mitigates collapse \\cite{model_collapse_avoidable,strong_model_collapse}.\nThese studies address \\emph{training-loop} degradation where parameter updates erode tail coverage; we study \\emph{inference chains} where parameters are fixed and only the input distribution evolves.\nTwo concurrent works are most directly relevant: \\citet{broken_telephone} analyze paraphrasing and translation chains, documenting progressive information loss, while \\citet{markovian_chains} demonstrate attractor dynamics that bound distinguishable depth to approximately three classes; \\citet{attractor_cycles} provide complementary evidence that LLM outputs converge to cyclic attractor states under repeated generation.\nWe study \\emph{non-paraphrastic continuation} chains, where the absence of semantic anchoring permits richer depth signal, and offer a complementary decomposition: where these works characterize \\emph{that} and \\emph{why} convergence occurs, DDR isolates \\emph{what} converges (NLL location) from what is preserved (NLL shape), enabling ordinal classification without per-generator calibration.\nThe independent convergence of all three approaches on ${\\sim}$3 distinguishable depth classes---via Markovian dynamics \\cite{markovian_chains}, information-theoretic loss \\cite{broken_telephone}, and distributional feature compression (this work)---points to a shared resolution ceiling in recursive text generation.\n\n\\subsection{Vocabulary Diversity and Stylometry}\n\\label{sec:related-diversity}\n\nDeclining lexical diversity in LLM text is documented in both training \\cite{curious_decline} and deployment settings \\cite{writing_diversity}.\nOur surface features (TTR, hapax ratio) are classical stylometric measures \\cite{stamatatos_survey,koppel_authorship}; we repurpose them from generator identity \\cite{uchendu_attribution} to the depth dimension, where their monotonic decline under top-$p$ sampling \\cite{nucleus_sampling} constitutes a drifting component of DDR.\n\n\\subsection{Data Quality and Provenance}\n\\label{sec:related-provenance}\n\nScalable data curation relies on quality signals \\cite{dsir,qurating,ccnet}; generation depth offers a complementary provenance axis.\nProactive watermarking \\cite{watermark_llm,synthid_text,aaronson_watermark} embeds identifiable patterns but degrades across multi-step chains \\cite{chainwash}, motivating passive approaches.\nOur NLL-based analysis connects to \\citet{min_k_percent} (probability-tail membership inference) but extends from binary membership to full distributional decomposition for ordinal depth.\n\\citet{koike_mia_detection} formalize this connection from the opposite direction, showing that detection scores implicitly perform membership inference, underscoring the shared statistical basis of detection and provenance.\n",
        "numLines": 34,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 34
      }
    },
